# Regulatory Watch — Cross-Country Reform Tracker

A single date-stamped registry of property-relevant reforms, transposition deadlines, ENDED programs, and watchlist items. Maintained as the **single source of truth for "what changed when"** — playbooks reference this; this references playbooks.

**Why it exists**: At 126 countries × 41 sections, the highest-blast-radius hallucination is asserting that an ENDED program is still active or quoting a tax rate that was reformed last quarter. This file makes "did anything change?" a single-file lookup before any re-stamp.

**Maintainer**: human + Claude. Each entry must have a primary-source URL, a verified date, and a next-revisit date.

---

## How to use this file

### Before producing any output

`--tax`, `--rental`, `--visa`, `--finance`, `--mains` (where regulated) sections must consult this file. If a relevant entry's `revisit_by` date has passed AND the playbook still asserts the pre-reform value, the section's confidence auto-downgrades (see `updater.md` § Auto-downgrade).

### Before re-stamping a section's `**Last verified**`

Check this file for entries touching the section since the prior stamp. If a reform is logged here that the playbook doesn't reflect, the section is **not yet re-verified** — patch the playbook first.

### When adding an entry

Required fields:
- `country` (ISO2)
- `topic` (one of: tax, rental, visa, finance, ownership, esg, cadastre, eurozone, other)
- `effective_date` (YYYY-MM-DD or "pending" + estimate)
- `enacted_date` (when the law/regulation was passed)
- `verified_date` (last time we confirmed status)
- `revisit_by` (next mandatory check)
- `source_url` (primary government / official journal preferred)
- `impact_tier` (1–4, see below)
- `playbook_sections_touched` (list of `--<flag>` flags)

---

## Impact tiers — how to rate a reform

The tier drives revisit cadence and anti-hallucination priority.

| Tier | Meaning | Revisit cadence | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — CRITICAL** | Reverses a prior assertion the skill makes (e.g., visa program ENDED, tax rate inverted, ownership opened/closed). Stale claim is a confident lie. | 90 days — full re-research from primary sources, not just a figure check | MT MEIN ECJ ENDED 29 Apr 2025 |
| **2 — MAJOR** | Material number change (>20% rate move), new mandatory diagnostic, EU directive transposition affecting many countries. | 90 days | RO VAT 19 → 21% Aug 2025; EPBD 2024/1275 |
| **3 — MEANINGFUL** | Threshold/credit/exemption tweak; parliamentary stage advance; rumored-to-draft transition. | 180 days | CY 2026 Comprehensive Tax Reform (CGT exemptions raised) |
| **4 — INCREMENTAL** | Indexation adjustment, fee tweak, technical correction, watchlist signal. | 365 days | LU Bëllegen Akt indexation; LV cadastral value rebase rumored |

Every entry below is **CONFIRMED CLOSED**. Listing any of these as active is the highest-stakes failure mode for `--visa`. Cross-check before any visa-section output.

| 🇧🇬 BG | CBI / fast-track | 18 Jun 2024 | Bulgarian parliament | none |
| 🇭🇺 HU | Residency Bond | 31 Mar 2017 | Programme expiration | Guest Investor 2024 (different) |

| 🇦🇺 AU | Significant Investor Visa (SIV / 188C) | 31 Jul 2024 | Migration program 2024-25 | none |
| 🇵🇹 PT | NHR (original 10-yr scheme) | 31 Dec 2023 (last applications) | OE 2024 | IFICI / NHR 2.0 — narrower, **pensions excluded** |
| 🇵🇹 PT | Golden Visa — ALL real-estate routes (residential + commercial) | 07 Oct 2023 | Lei 56/2023 (Mais Habitação) | GV via non-real-estate funds / research / culture / company + jobs only |
| 🇲🇪 ME | Citizenship by Investment | 31 Dec 2022 | Programme expiration | none |
| 🇦🇱 AL | CBI (proposed but never operational) | n/a | Never enacted | n/a — do not list as available |

**Rule**: If a user asks "what visa programs are open in <country>", consult this table first; only entries NOT here are candidates for "open" status, and even those need a fresh `verified_date` ≤180 days.

- `2025-09-22 | rental | Madrid STR freeze SUPERSEDED by a permanent zoning regime — do not describe Madrid as frozen. MADRID: the April 2024 licence freeze was an express art. 70 LSCM cautionary measure ("Suspensión temporal de nuevas licencias ... es una medida cautelar adoptada antes de que se defina la futura ordenación de las VUT", Ayuntamiento de Madrid), and art. 70.4 LSCM caps such a suspension at "no podrá exceder de un año", extendable one further year — it cannot still be running in 2026. It was replaced by the Modificación del PGOU "para la mejora del uso residencial" (expediente 135-2024-00206, Plan RESIDE), definitively approved by Acuerdo del Consejo de Gobierno de la Comunidad de Madrid of 27 Aug 2025 (BOCM 211 of 4 Sep 2025); normative text in BOCM 226 of 22 Sep 2025, which the Ayuntamiento transparency portal labels normativa aplicable. New art. 7.6.3 bis binds ONLY the APE 00.01 Centro Histórico (ámbito per art. 4.3.17). Inside it: hospedaje in part of an EXISTING RESIDENTIAL building is barred on every floor (para. 10.b.i "No se admite su implantación en ninguna de las plantas del edificio") — dispersed VUT in central residential buildings is permanently barred, not paused; in existing NON-residential buildings hospedaje stays admissible as a complementary use (any situation at levels A and B, upper floors only at C and D, para. 10.a). Edificio exclusivo then varies by the art. 8.1.29 use-compatibility level — levels A and B (para. 8): on parcels with an existing non-residential building plain "uso alternativo" (plan especial de protección if catalogued level 1 or 2); on unbuilt parcels or existing residential buildings VUT is "uso alternativo" only in buildings catalogued protection level 3, the activity licence lapses after "quince (15) años" back to vivienda colectiva and that condition "se harán constar en el Registro de la Propiedad", autorizable at protection levels 1 or 2 via plan especial, and "Uso prohibido en parcelas no edificadas y en edificios no catalogados"; levels C and D (para. 9): plain "uso alternativo", plan especial only if catalogued level 1 or 2, with NO 15-year lapse and NO Registro annotation; level E (para. 11) and Colonias Históricas (para. 6.b): hospedaje not admitted. Áreas de Planeamiento (API, APE, APR) interior to the APE take the A and B conditions (para. 6.a). The level map sits in art. 8.1.29, which BOCM 226 does NOT republish — establish the parcel's level before relying on any of the above. Citywide art. 7.6.3.4: locales in residential buildings "contarán siempre con acceso independiente". Disposición derogatoria única folds the 2019 Plan Especial de Hospedaje (PE 00/313, approved 27 Mar 2019, BOCM 23-04-2019) into art. 7.6.3 bis. Litigation status not verified against a primary court source for this entry — treat annulment risk as unassessed. Ajuntament announced 21 Jun 2024 (Pla Viure) that it will grant none: Consorci de l'Habitatge de Barcelona, 21 Jun 2024 — "al cap de cinc anys des de la seva entrada en vigor, tots els HUT de la ciutat desapareixeran i quedaran prohibits. There is also no municipal decree — the only decree in the chain is the Generalitat's; 21 Jun 2024 was a municipal announcement applying it. Stock 10,101 per Ajuntament Mesura de Govern per a la Gestió Turística 2024-2027 — "Es preveu que l'any 2028, els 10.101 HUT actuals perdin la seva llicència" (2024 data). DT2 apartat 2 (cite as DT2.2, NOT DT1.2 — DT1 defines qualifying municipalities) allows ONE owner-requested extension of up to five further years, to 2033, where the holder proves the transitional regime does not compensate the loss of the enabling title — "pot sol·licitar una única ampliació del règim transitori de fins a cinc anys més", to be requested at the ajuntament after four years from entry into force and before the apartat 1 transitional period ends. Constitutional challenge FAILED — STC 64/2025 de 13 de marzo (BOE-A-2025-7430) "desestima en su totalidad el recurso de inconstitucionalidad" against arts. 1-3, DT1 apartat 1 a), DT2, DF1 and the annex. - `2019-03-27 (norms absorbed into art. 7.6.3 bis by BOCM 22 Sep 2025) | rental | Madrid PEH — legacy entry CORRECTED and NARROWED; two limbs WITHDRAWN as unsourced. DATE and SCOPE were wrong: the Plan Especial de Hospedaje is a 2019 instrument covering ELEVEN districts, not a 2018 Distrito Centro plan. Disposición derogatoria única of BOCM 226 of 22 Sep 2025 anuncio 47 names it verbatim "Plan Especial de Regulación del Uso de Servicios Terciarios en la clase de Hospedaje. Distritos de Centro, Arganzuela, Retiro, Salamanca, Chamartín, Tetuán, Chamberí, Moncloa-Aravaca, Latina, Carabanchel y Usera, aprobado definitivamente el 27 de marzo de 2019 y publicado en el BOCM el 23-04-2019 (PE 00/313)" — that eleven-district list is the REPEALED plan's scope, NOT a current perimeter. NOT A LIVE INSTRUMENT: its norms "quedan incorporadas, con las adaptaciones necesarias, en el texto del artículo 7.6.3 bis de las Normas Urbanísticas del PG97" and "Quedan sin efecto los documentos de Diagnóstico y Memoria del citado Plan Especial" — never cite the PEH as current. The operative regime is the 2025-09-22 Madrid entry above and is deliberately NOT restated here; note only that art. 7.6.3 bis.2 fixes its own ámbito as "el del Área de Planeamiento Específico del Centro Histórico (APE 00.01) definido en el artículo 4.3.17" and art. 4.3.17 is not reproduced in this anuncio, so the perimeter cannot be resolved from it — the string Distrito Centro does not appear in the anuncio at all. WITHDRAWN as unsourced: ">90 nights/yr classed as commercial use". Full-text searches of both instruments run 2026-08-07 find no nights-per-year or days-per-year threshold: in the anuncio, días, noches, al año and pernocta are absent and the single occurrence of 90 is "unidades menores de 90 m² útiles"; in Decreto 79/2014 as amended by DECRETO 27/2026 de 25 de marzo (BOCM 80 of 6 Abr 2026, "entrará en vigor a los veinte días siguientes al de su publicación", so in force since late April 2026) al año, anuales and noventa are absent from both texts. The only period rule in the 2014 original was a per-stay floor in old art. 17.3 ("no podrán contratarse por un período inferior a cinco días"), which is not a per-year cap and which the 2026 decree does not carry forward when it rewrites art. 17 wholesale ("Nueve. El artículo 17 queda redactado del siguiente modo"). The 2026 decree instead empowers municipalities to limit "el número máximo de viviendas de uso turístico por edificio, al ámbito, zona, sector o períodos", so any day-count could only sit in a municipal limit adopted under that delegation — data not publicly available in the sources checked; verify at Ayuntamiento de Madrid and the Comunidad de Madrid dirección general competente en materia de Turismo. ALSO WITHDRAWN: "portal/lift separation from residents" — portal does not occur in the anuncio and the single ascensor occurrence is an unrelated rehabilitation rule ("obras de acondicionamiento y/o de reestructuración puntual para implantación de ascensores"). What IS sourced is weaker and citywide, not Distrito-Centro-specific: art. 7.6.3.4 "Los locales integrados en edificios de uso residencial, con independencia de su modalidad y situación, contarán siempre con acceso independiente". The stronger wording "acceso independiente desde el exterior, sin utilizar elementos comunes de circulación del edificio" is art. 7.1.4 apartado 4 and is expressly displaced for this use class — "Las condiciones de acceso independiente de los locales de la clase de uso terciario hospedaje serán las establecidas en el artículo 7.6.3.4 de estas normas" — so do not attribute a common-stair or lift exclusion to hospedaje. TIER LOWERED 2 to 3: with both limbs withdrawn this line no longer records a reform and no longer reverses a live regime assertion (the supersession is carried by the 2025-09-22 Tier-1 entry); what remains is a date and scope correction plus two withdrawals. De-minimis is CUMULATIVE AVAILABILITY, not one block — rates are unaffected "unless you only make your property available for short-term visitors for up to 28 days", and "These 28 days can include multiple stays by different guests and refers to the total length of time the property is available to be rented" (the Mar-2024 QLDC factsheet's "once a year for a single period of up to 28 consecutive days" is same-issuer but older — superseded by the live page). The 90-night cap is ZONE-SPECIFIC, not district-wide: verified verbatim only for Lower Density Suburban Residential std 7.5.18.1 "The total nights of occupation by paying guests on a site do not exceed a cumulative total of 90 nights per annum from the date of initial registration" and the same 90 in ch.8 Medium Density Residential (PDP Decisions Version Jun 2023); QLDC publishes a separate standards sheet per zone and states the rules "will depend on which District Plan Zone your property lies within" — never assume 90 without checking the parcel's zone. Registration is mandatory and "if you wish to operate beyond the permitted standards for your zone, then a resource consent will be required from QLDC before operating". V supuestos ("Departamento, casas y villas particulares, total o parcialmente") at 6%, computed by whoever collects the consideration, "ya sea el Anfitrion o la plataforma tecnologica o digital" - both platform-tied, because art. 2 fr. VISITAX is NOT "$5 USD per visitor": Ley de Derechos art. 51-Octies charges foreign visitors 2.50 UMA on entry, exempting those entering by the southern border, and carries no reform stamp later than its POE 23-12-2022 addition in the POE 18-12-2025 consolidation. The five-dollar charge is a SEPARATE art. 51-Nonies derecho on cruise passengers only (added POE 21-12-2023, collected and withheld by the naviera at ticket purchase, crew exempt, charged only on the first port of call), and paying it credits against the 51-Octies derecho. - `2026-07-01 | finance | NBG de-dollarization ceiling on unhedged foreign-currency loans rose to GEL 1,000,000 effective 1 Jul 2026 (in force on 2026-08-07), after GEL 500,000 to GEL 750,000 effective 1 Aug 2025 — FSC decisions read verbatim "the limit on unhedged foreign currency loans will increase from 500,000 GEL to 750,000 GEL, effective from August 1, 2025" and "the limit on unhedged foreign currency loans increases from 750,000 GEL to 1,000,000 GEL, effective from July 1, 2026"; below that ceiling the loan is issued in lari unless the borrower's income is in the loan currency (licensed-lender restatement, Terabank: "A loan or bank credit for amounts less than 1 000 000 GEL can only be issued in the national currency, except when the borrower's income and the loan are in the same currency"). CORRECTION to the prior entry — the FX-mortgage LTV cap was NOT tightened and NBG keys these rules off loan currency versus income currency, never buyer residency: FSR 2025 reads "since 2019, the National Bank of Georgia determined a maximum LTV ratio of 70 percent for mortgage loans issued in foreign currency", and NBG's live PTI and LTV table still reads LTV 90% for GEL loans and 70% for foreign-currency loans (checked 2026-08-07). The only LTV move in the window was a LOOSENING that HELPED buyers with income from abroad — Feb 2025 FSC "temporarily increases the maximum loan-to-value (LTV) ratio by 5 percentage points to 90% for local currency loans secured by real estate, issued to natural persons. Additionally, for individuals receiving income from abroad, the LTV ratio for mortgage loans will be raised by 10 percentage points to 80%". Same-tier NBG conflict to carry: the FSR 2025 macroprudential-measures table stamps the foreign-currency LTV row at 80% dated 26.02.2025 while the live PTI and LTV page reads 70% — treat 70% as the standing general cap and 80% as the income-from-abroad carve-out, and confirm the applicable figure with the lender before budgeting a deposit. Rate path is tightening, not post-EUR-cycle normalization: MPC raised the refinancing rate 0.25pp to 8.25% on 6 May 2026 and held at 8.25% on 17 Jun, 29 Jul and 9 Sep 2026, against a 3% inflation target (Sep 2026 MPC: headline inflation 5.6% in Aug 2026, core 3.6%, next MPC 21 Oct 2026). ### 🇮🇩 ID

- `2021-02 | foreign-buyer | PP No. 18/2021 (ditetapkan and in force 2 Feb 2021) — Hak Pakai (a right of use, not freehold) is the direct land right open to Orang Asing (Pasal 49(2)(e)); term on Tanah Negara / Tanah Hak Pengelolaan is 30 + 20 extension + 30 renewal = up to 80 yr (Pasal 52(1)), but Hak Pakai over Hak Milik is max 30 yr, renewable only by a new PPAT deed (Pasal 52(3)). Eligibility is a "dokumen keimigrasian" (Pasal 69(1)), which the Penjelasan defines as "visa, paspor, atau izin tinggal" — KITAS/KITAP is NOT a precondition in the PP text itself (Permen ATR/BPN 18/2021 implementing conditions not read in primary — confirm at the Kantor Pertanahan). Pasal 1 angka 14 defines Orang Asing as a non-citizen "yang keberadaannya memberikan manfaat, melakukan usaha, bekerja, atau berinvestasi di Indonesia", and Pasal 50 obliges a Hak Pakai holder who no longer meets Pasal 49 to release or transfer the right within 1 year, failing which it is extinguished by law. Foreigners may hold Hak Milik atas Satuan Rumah Susun in rumah susun on Hak Pakai OR HGB land (Pasal 71(1)(b)), limited by Pasal 71(2) to KEK, free-trade/free-port zones, kawasan industri and "kawasan ekonomi lainnya"; Pasal 72 limits foreign holdings by minimum price, plot area, number of plots/units and residential use | PP 18/2021 official text via JDIH Kota Surabaya `https://jdih.surabaya.go.id/uploads/peraturan/3815_pp_18-2021.pdf` + Ministry of ATR/BPN | 2026-09-24 | 2026-12-23 | 1 | --tax --visa ownership`
- `ongoing | foreign-buyer | Nominee ownership is void by statute, not merely risky — UU 5/1960 (UUPA) Art. 26(2), text as published on the Ministry ATR/BPN JDIH portal, makes any act intended to transfer hak milik to a foreigner directly or indirectly "adalah batal karena hukum dan tanahnya jatuh kepada Negara" (void by operation of law, and the land falls to the State), with Art. 27(a)(4) extinguishing the hak milik outright — so the foreign payer's outlay is not secured by the land it bought. The statute's own elucidation confirms the legitimate route: "hanya warganegara Indonesia saja yang dapat mempunyai hak milik atas tanah … Orang-orang asing dapat mempunyai tanah dengan hak pakai yang luasnya terbatas". Kanwil BPN Provinsi Bali records ~463 Hak Pakai parcels held in WNA names Bali-wide (regulator's own wording "sekitar 463", on data through Mar 2025), each generally not exceeding 5,000 m2, and states nominee practice "bersifat tersembunyi dan umumnya baru dapat terungkap ketika muncul persoalan hukum" (hidden, generally surfacing only once a legal problem arises) because the paperwork formally sits in an Indonesian citizen's name. SUPERSEDES the prior wording: no Indonesian court is named "Bali Provincial Court" (Bali's general courts are Pengadilan Negeri Denpasar and Pengadilan Tinggi Denpasar), and the asserted 2023-2025 nominee voidings were never verified — the Direktori Putusan at putusan3.mahkamahagung.go.id returns HTTP 403 to automated retrieval (re-confirmed 2026-08-07), so any circulating 2023-2025 nominee case count is unverified and must be checked manually against the docket | Kementerian ATR/BPN JDIH UU 5/1960 `https://jdih.atrbpn.go.id/peraturan/download/32/UU_05_1960.pdf` + Kanwil BPN Provinsi Bali `https://bali.atrbpn.go.id/berita/kepala-kantor-wilayah-bpn-provinsi-bali-klarifikasi-terkait-isu-pulau-di-bali-yang-dikuasai-wna` | 2026-09-24 | 2026-12-23 | 1 | --tax ownership`

- `2025-10-10 | rental | SUPERSEDED CITATION, not a substantive reversal — Permenparekraf 4/2021, which the ID playbook --rental headline rule cites (playbook line 332, grep-verified 2026-08-07) as the operative sectoral standard, has been revoked. Permenpar 6/2025 (Standar Kegiatan Usaha, Tata Cara Pelaksanaan Pengawasan, dan Sanksi Administratif pada Penyelenggaraan Perizinan Berusaha Berbasis Risiko Sektor Pariwisata) Pasal 22, in BAB VI Ketentuan Penutup, reads verbatim "... Nomor 4 Tahun 2021 tentang Standar Kegiatan Usaha pada Penyelenggaraan Perizinan Berusaha Berbasis Risiko Sektor Pariwisata (Berita Negara Republik Indonesia Tahun 2021 Nomor 283) dan ... Nomor 8 Tahun 2021 tentang Sanksi Administratif Perizinan Berusaha Berbasis Risiko Sektor Pariwisata dan Ekonomi Kreatif (Berita Negara Republik Indonesia Tahun 2021 Nomor 682), dicabut dan dinyatakan tidak berlaku", and Pasal 23 "Peraturan Menteri ini mulai berlaku pada tanggal diundangkan". The salinan carries "Ditetapkan di Jakarta pada tanggal 10 Oktober 2025"; its Diundangkan date did not render legibly in the copy read here, so the in-force date is taken from BPK JDIH, whose CATATAN reads "Peraturan Menteri Pariwisata ini mulai berlaku pada tanggal 10 Oktober 2025" (BN 2025 No. 793). WHERE THE STANDARD NOW SITS: in the national ministerial Lampiran, which carries "STANDAR KEGIATAN USAHA VILLA TINGKAT RISIKO MENENGAH RENDAH NO. KBLI: (55193) VILLA" and a parallel "NO. KBLI: (55130) PONDOK WISATA" standard, with compliance evidenced by a "Sertifikat Standar Usaha Villa Dengan Tingkat Risiko Menengah Rendah ... yang diberikan oleh Lembaga Sertifikasi Usaha/Lembaga Sertifikasi Produk Bidang Pariwisata", a third-party conformity body ("lembaga penilaian kesesuaian milik pihak ketiga"). This confirms from the primary text the KBLI 55193 villa code the ID block already uses at playbook line 336. It speaks to where the standard is SET, not to how it is enforced locally, about which this entry now makes no claim. TWO DATES TO DIARY: Pasal 5(2) requires the Sertifikat Standar Usaha Pariwisata "paling lambat 1 (satu) tahun terhitung sejak PB terverifikasi", Pasal 5(3) excepting certain "kegiatan usaha dengan tingkat Risiko menengah rendah tertentu"; and Pasal 21 gives operators whose Risk tier changed "jangka waktu paling lama 1 (satu) tahun terhitung sejak diundangkannya Peraturan Menteri ini", i.e. to 10 Oct 2026. The transitional article immediately preceding Pasal 21 keeps pre-existing OSS-issued PB and PB UMKU that were verified, approved and still valid before promulgation usable. WITHDRAWN — the prior wording of this line, that Bali villa STR enforcement "varies by kabupaten" and that Pondok Wisata licensing is "patchy", is not carried forward: no primary source is cited for it, and per-kabupaten STR enforcement activity and Pondok Wisata licence-issuance counts are data not publicly available — verify per property with the DPMPTSP and the Dinas Pariwisata of the specific kabupaten or kota where it sits, and against the OSS record for the business itself. DELIBERATELY NOT ASSERTED, in either direction: whether the legacy TDUP instrument survives the risk-based regime, whether Permenpar 6/2025 alters the nationality restriction on Pondok Wisata, and how the parent statute UU 10/2009 should now be cited. None was re-derived in this pass, so do not restate any of them on the strength of this entry. - `2025-07-01 | rental | Bali Foreign Tourist Levy IDR 150,000 cashless e-Payment only, amount unchanged since the 14 Feb 2024 launch and reconfirmed in Perda Bali 6/2023 Pasal 5(2) as amended by Perda Bali 2/2025 (ditetapkan + diundangkan Denpasar 20 Jun 2025, in force 1 Jul 2025, Lembaran Daerah 2025 No. 2, status Berlaku on peraturan.go.id). Levy is due on EACH entry to Bali "secara langsung dari luar negeri atau secara tidak langsung melalui wilayah lainnya di Indonesia" (Pasal 4A(1)), but one payment covers that visit: Pasal 5(1) runs "selama Wisatawan Asing berwisata ke Bali dan sebelum meninggalkan wilayah Indonesia" (carried over from old Pasal 5(2), not a 2025 change), and a duplicate paid on the same arrival date is refundable under Pergub 25/2025 Pasal 15A. Payment DEADLINE relaxed, not the pre-payment option — advance payment was always allowed: old Pasal 5(4) required payment "sebelum atau pada saat memasuki pintu kedatangan di Bali", new Pasal 5(3) allows "sebelum atau selama berwisata di Bali", and Pergub Bali 25/2025 Pasal 12(1) lists arrival by air-sea-land, during-stay, OR "keberangkatan Wisatawan Asing keluar Bali" (departure) as payment points. New Pasal 4A(2) EXEMPTS transport crew, KITAS/KITAP holders, diplomatic and service visas, family-reunification visas, student visas, golden visas, and "pemegang jenis visa lainnya" — an open catch-all with detail delegated to a Pergub by Pasal 4A(4), so treat the list as open, not closed; the KITAS/KITAP exemption is the material one for resident foreign owners. Sanctions RESTRUCTURED rather than tightened: old Pasal 6(2) flatly provided that non-payers "dilarang berwisata ke Bali"; new BAB VIIIA Pasal 16A substitutes graduated administrative sanctions (teguran lisan, teguran tertulis, and-or "tidak mendapatkan pelayanan di Daya Tarik Wisata"). Procedure is Pergub Bali 2/2024 ONLY as amended by Pergub Bali 25/2025 (ditetapkan 20 Jun 2025, in force 1 Jul 2025, Berita Daerah 2025 No. 28), which amends Pasal 1 angka 11-12, 4, 9(2), 12, 13, 14(1) and 14(4), 16(1), inserts Pasal 15A and BAB IVA Pasal 22A-22C Imbal Jasa capped "paling tinggi 3%" of levy transactions, and deletes BAB V and Pasal 23 — citing Perda 2/2025 Pasal II(1) alone points at superseded procedure text. - `2026-02-24 | rental | Bali Perda 4/2026 tentang Pengendalian Alih Fungsi Lahan Produktif dan Larangan Alih Kepemilikan Lahan Secara Nomine — ditetapkan and diundangkan Denpasar 24 Feb 2026, in force on promulgation (Pasal 26), LD Provinsi Bali 2026 No. 4, signed Wayan Koster. WHAT IT PROHIBITS: Pasal 13(2) bars conversion of Lahan Produktif designated in the spatial plan (exceptions in 13(3) for national strategic policy, public-interest land acquisition and disaster relocation); Pasal 14 bars any person from acting as "perantara, fasilitator, atau menyediakan sarana yang menyebabkan warga negara asing menguasai kepemilikan lahan secara Nomine", Nomine being defined (Pasal 1 angka 11) as an arrangement naming someone to hold or manage land rights on behalf of a foreign national as beneficial owner. SANCTIONS ARE ADMINISTRATIVE: Pasal 20(2) lists written warning, temporary suspension, site closure, licence revocation or annulment, demolition, restoration of land function, incentive withdrawal and administrative fine; Pasal 20(5) adds only that criminal sanctions may apply "sesuai dengan ketentuan peraturan perundang-undangan". The Perda does not itself create a criminal offence or a prison term for nominee arrangements. THE 5-YEAR + IDR 1bn FIGURE is UU 41/2009 Pasal 72(1), for an individual converting designated LP2B land contrary to Pasal 44(1); UU 41/2009 appears in the Perda's Mengingat (legal basis), not in its sanctions article, and it is a land-conversion penalty, not a nominee penalty (corporate officers: Pasal 74, 2-7 yr + IDR 2-7bn). - `2025-07-01 | tax | PIT scale 0/10/20% took effect for the income year commencing 1 Jul 2025 (Finance Act 2025) and is still the scale MRA administers as at 2026-08-07 — the MRA PAYE table reads First Rs 500,000 at 0%, Next Rs 500,000 at 10%, Remainder 20%, and employees with monthly emoluments not exceeding Rs 38,462 are not subject to PAYE (company director and board fees excepted). CORRECTION to this entry's prior wording: the scale did NOT replace a "15% flat" rate. MRA's own Budget 2025/26 Fiscal Measures Highlights prints the outgoing scale beside the new one as ELEVEN bands — First Rs 390,000 at 0%, then 2%, 4%, 6%, 8%, 10%, 12%, 14%, 16%, 18%, remainder 20% — and states "The new income tax rates and bands will be effective as from income year starting on 01st July 2025". That eleven-band scale is the FA-2023 one effective from 1 Jul 2023, whose MRA communique of 08 Jun 2023 also records "The solidarity levy has been abolished". Budget Speech 2025-2026 para 261: "Regarding personal income tax, we are reducing the tax bands from eleven to three". ALSO LIVE: Fair Share Contribution — MRA states an individual pays FSC "in addition to his liability to income tax where his income threshold exceeds Rs 12 million in an income year", collected under PAYE "as from 01 July 2025 ... up to 30 June 2028", the employer withholding 15% of monthly emoluments exceeding Rs 923,077 (Rs 12,000,000 divided by 13), so the top marginal load exceeds 20%. NOT ESTABLISHED: Budget Speech 2026-2027 para 280 announces a further restructure — 20% on the portion of chargeable income "exceeding Rs 1 million up to Rs 12 million", 35% above Rs 12 million, and "this tax band will replace the fair share contribution for individuals" — but whether that measure is enacted or in force for the income year commencing 1 Jul 2026 is data not publicly available — verify at MRA and the Attorney-General's statute portal before relying on either scale for income year 2026-27. Two earlier drafts of this entry were refuted for asserting a parliamentary status that could not be sourced; no status claim is made here. | MRA Budget 2025/26 Fiscal Measures Highlights `https://www.mra.mu/download/BudgetHighlights2025.pdf` + MRA PAYE `https://www.mra.mu/index.php/employers/paye` + MRA Communique 08 Jun 2023 `https://www.mra.mu/download/CommuniqueBudgetMeasures080623.pdf` + Budget Speech 2025-2026 `https://mof.govmu.org/Documents/Budget2025_26/2025_26_budgetSpeech.pdf` + Budget Speech 2026-2027 `https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Budget-Speech-2026-2027.pdf` + 2026-27 enactment unverified, check `https://lawsofmauritius.govmu.org` | 2026-08-07 | 2027-02-03 | 3 | --tax`

- `2024-12-13 | foreign-buyer | 85/15 currency rule IN FORCE since 13 Dec 2024 (the Feb 2025 date is only the EDB FAQ explainer's own stamp, not the amendments' date). EDB (Smart City Scheme / Property Development Scheme / Invest Hotel Scheme / Real Estate Development Scheme) (Amendment No. 2) Regulations 2024 (Cabinet took note 6 Dec 2024, in force 13 Dec 2024) require a non-citizen acquiring residential property under IRS/RES/IHS/PDS/SCS, after transferring funds from abroad in hard convertible currency, to pay 85% of the purchase price to the promoter in Mauritius rupee with the remaining 15% in USD/EUR/any hard convertible FX or MUR; the notary receives the price in FX and effects the 85/15 split on transfer to the promoter. Local-loan floor raised USD 500,000 → USD 750,000: where the price exceeds USD 750,000 the first USD 750,000 must be own funds transferred to Mauritius and paid in MUR to the promoter, with a Mauritian bank loan only for the balance and loan repayment in hard convertible FX. Per EDB FAQ the amendments apply to a first sale only (resales out of scope) and NOT to Ground+2 apartments; test is a sales deed signed on or after 13 Dec 2024 (pre-13-Dec deeds with VEFA tranche payments still falling due are treated separately in the FAQ — confirm exact wording with the notary). Resident carve-out (FAQ Q13/Q16): a non-citizen holding an Occupation Permit / retired or other Residence Permit may use funds previously transferred from abroad, and one earning MUR income in Mauritius may finance the whole acquisition in MUR; residents may still borrow locally only above the first USD 750,000 of own funds. No repealing amendment found (EDB newsroom notice + FAQ still published 2026-09-24). ### 🇧🇸 BS

- `2022-01-01 | tax | VAT standard rate cut 12%→10% effective 1 Jan 2022 (DIR VAT Guidance on 2021 Amendments) — a reduction, not an increase; 10% standard rate still in force as at the FY2026/27 Budget Communication (27 May 2026) and applies to property-related supplies incl. commercial rent and explicit HOA/management fees (DIR VAT FAQ); unprepared/uncooked grocery food removed from VAT 1 Apr 2026 does not affect property costs | DIR Department of Inland Revenue + `https://inlandrevenue.finance.gov.bs/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/VAT-Guidance-2021-Amendments.pdf` | 2026-09-24 | 2026-12-23 | 2 | --tax`
- `2026-07-01 in force (Act No. 21 of 2026); prior amendment 2025-07-01 (Act No. 43 of 2025) | tax | ENACTED: the Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2026 (No. 21 of 2026, assent 30 Jun 2026, Extraordinary Official Gazette 1 Jul 2026, s.1(2) "This Act shall come into force on the 1 day of July, 2026") enacted the former Bill 2026-0028 and amends the Real Property Tax Act Ch. 375 as previously amended by Act No. 43 of 2025. (1) New "foreign owner-occupied property" class: property owned by a non-Bahamian who holds a residence permit or annual work permit under the Immigration Act (Ch. 191) or a Home Owner Resident Permit under the International Persons Landholding Act (Ch. 140) and "occupies and resides in such property exclusively as a dwelling house on a permanent or seasonal basis" — taxed at "five-eighths of one per centum" (0.625%) on the part of the value up to $1,000,000 and 1% on the part above $1,000,000, with the maximum annual tax not to exceed $200,000 (no exempt first slice in the Act text). (2) "owner-occupied property" is restricted to a "Bahamian or the holder of certificate of permanent residence with a right to engage in gainful occupation", and the words "for a period of not less than 90 days in the year" are deleted. (3) The owner-occupied cap proviso is replaced by "the maximum annual tax rate shall not exceed one hundred and fifty thousand dollars" — the 183-day condition that the 2025 Act attached to the cap is gone. (4) s.42A(1) first-time-homeowner exemption now reads "Bahamian" person; s.42A(6) figure $500,000.00 → $600,000.00 — for a foreign buyer the Bahamian-only restriction is the operative change. (5) New s.3A: jointly owned property whose owners fall in different categories is apportioned equally and each share is taxed under its own owner's category. (6) New ss.25A–25Y power of sale: the Treasurer may sell property whose tax has been in arrears for more than twelve months, and these sections "shall not apply to owner-occupied property beneficially owned by a Bahamian" — foreign-owned property is within scope. The FY2026/27 Budget Communication's "former 180-day test" wording never had a statutory counterpart (the 2025 Act said 90 and 183) — do not carry 180 days. The official tabled-bills page still lists "Real Property Tax (Amendment) Bill, 2026" (a list of bills tabled in 2026, not evidence of non-enactment). WARNING: DIR RPT FAQ Q10 still lists only owner-occupied (1st $300,000 exempt / 0.625% next $200,000 / 1% above $500,000), residential, commercial-or-foreign-owned-rental and vacant-land-for-foreigners bands, with no foreign owner-occupied category — DIR guidance is behind the 2026 Act, so confirm classification and bands with DIR in writing before any carry-cost or transaction-cost computation | Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2026 No. 21 of 2026 https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2026/2026-0021A/2026-0021A.pdf + Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2025 No. 43 of 2025 https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2025/2025-0043A/2025-0043A.pdf + consolidated index https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/index/by-alphabetical.html + tabled bills https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/tabled-in-parliament/tabled-in-house-of-assembly.html + FY2026-27 Budget Communication https://bahamasbudget.gov.bs + DIR RPT FAQ https://inlandrevenue.finance.gov.bs/real-property-tax/faqs-rpt/ | 2026-09-24 | 2026-12-23 | 1 | --tax --home-tax`

- `2025-10-30 | foreign-buyer | CCJ ENDED its decade-long monitoring of the 2015 Consent Order at the Final Monitoring Hearing on 30 Oct 2025. CCJ President Winston Anderson: "That supervision ends today", citing the January 2022 Maya of Southern Belize Free Prior and Informed Consultation Protocol and "the adoption in September 2025 of a working draft on Recognition and Protection of Maya Customary Land Tenure in Southern Belize, along with the formulation of drafting instructions and the selection/engagement of a draftsperson". The Court closed its MONITORING role only, not its jurisdiction — "The Court will, of course, remain of service to both sides in its regular capacity". Subject-specific legislation is still NOT enacted: the Court called enacted legislation "the ultimate goal" and said its hope is that implementation of the 2015 Order "will proceed through its legislative and other phases", and no Maya, indigenous or customary-land-tenure Bill appears in the National Assembly Draft Bills 2026 register (16 bills listed, register checked 2026-08-07; the nationalassembly.gov.bz host returned 'Account Suspended' on 2026-09-24, so the register could not be re-checked). On 30 Jul 2026 the CCJ dismissed a Toledo Alcaldes Association / Maya Leaders Alliance application in BZCV2014/002 seeking clarification of the 2015 Consent Order — Justice Anderson: "The precise scope and extent of Maya Customary Land Tenure will therefore necessarily will remain inchoate and uncertain until authoritatively identified and codified by the laws of Belize", and the Court did not think it "ought to intervene at this point in the way asked for by the applicants", noting other routes to challenge the final product (⚠️ 7News Belize hearing transcript 30 Jul 2026 — secondary; CCJ written decision not read). Consistent with that, Minister of Indigenous Peoples Affairs Dr Louis Zabaneh, at the second National Consultation and Information-Sharing Session in Punta Gorda Town, said draft legislation built on a Cabinet-approved policy "now goes through a review panel where we have membership from the government on the panel, from the MLA /TAA and from private land owners" before returning to Cabinet and only then going to the National Assembly (⚠️ broadcast-media report dated 5 Aug 2026 quoting the responsible minister — secondary tier, not a GoB publication). - `2001-08-30 | foreign-buyer | ENFORCEMENT-TREND CLAIM WITHDRAWN and replaced with the statutory position. Two verification passes (2026-08-07) located no MLMUPC or GDCG prakas, circular or statement evidencing a 2024-2026 anti-nominee campaign, and the US State Department 2025 Investment Climate Statement for Cambodia does not use the word nominee anywhere in the report as fetched 2026-08-07 — so "under enforcement pressure" and "increasingly challenged" were unsourced and are removed. The statute is the stronger claim and is primary-quotable. Constitution Art. 44: "Only natural persons or legal entities of Khmer nationality shall have the right to own land." Land Law 2001 (Royal Kram NS/RKM/0801/14, promulgated 30 August 2001) Art. 9 expressly PERMITS a company with 51 percent or more Cambodian-held shares to own land, but adds: "Only percentages stipulated in the articles of incorporation are taken into account. Any private agreement signed by a shareholder that is contrary to this article is null and void." The side-agreement, share-pledge and power-of-attorney bundle marketed as making a nominee land-holding company safe for the foreign funder is therefore VOID BY STATUTE from the day it is signed, not merely exposed to enforcement risk — the structure is legal, the foreign buyer's protection inside it is not. Land Law Art. 8: a foreigner who "falsifies national identity to become an owner of land in Cambodia shall be punished as determined under article 251" (Art. 251: "imprisonment from one (1) to five (5) years") and "Any property bought under these circumstances will be seized as state property without compensation from the state." CITE Land Law Art. 9, NOT Sub-Decree 111 Art. 3.2 — Sub-Decree 111 (2005) is a QIP-incentives instrument and its successor Sub-Decree 139 of 26 June 2023 carries no Use of Nominees clause. WATCH: the 2025 ICS states "Cambodia is drafting a new land law that is still undergoing stakeholder consultation" — no draft text published, no forecast made | Constitution of the Kingdom of Cambodia Art. 44 via Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation `https://mfaic.gov.kh/publish/files/uploads/2Z87AYOO63VI/Constitution%20of%20the%20Kingdom%20of%20Cambodia.pdf` + Land Law 2001 Arts. 8, 9 and 251 via `https://asset.cambodia.gov.kh/mowa/2016/12/land-law-2001-kh-eng1.pdf` (unofficial English translation, Khmer version official) + Council for the Development of Cambodia `https://cdc.gov.kh/laws-and-regulations/land` + Sub-Decree 139 of 26 June 2023 `https://cib-cdc.gov.kh/media/2025/04/2.-Sub-Decree-139-on-Implementing-LOI-2023-EN.pdf` + US State Department 2025 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2025-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` | 2026-09-24 | 2026-12-23 | 1 | --visa ownership --integrity`

- `2022-10-20 | tax | Capital Gains Tax 10% — Law N° 014/2025 of 27/05/2025 (Official Gazette n° Special of 29/05/2025, in force from gazettement) amended Art. 36 of Law N° 027/2022: "The capital gain tax rate is 10% of the capital gain". CGT base per amended Art. 37 = direct or indirect sale or transfer of shares, licences, debt instruments, options, guarantees and similar assets — immovable property is NOT in the CGT base. Correction to the prior framing: Art. 36 of Law N° 027/2022 as promulgated read five percent (5%), and Law N° 051/2023 of 05/09/2023 amended only Arts. 9 + 48 (CIT 30% → 28%) without touching CGT, so "5% flat" WAS the primary statute for 20/10/2022–28/05/2025. Gains on commercial immovable property fall into business profit (Art. 19, "proceeds of sale of any business asset"), taxed at CIT 28% (Art. 48 as amended by Law 051/2023) or PIT progressive to 30% (Art. 14 top band) — not a 30% CIT. Residential individual sales remain captured through the 2%/2.5% immovable-property sale levy (RWF 5M exempt floor). ### 🇹🇹 TT

- `2024 (enforced); halted 2025 | tax | Property Tax reintroduced 2024 and then HALTED by the 2025 government. Statutory residential rate is 2% of Annual Taxable Value (ATV = Annual Rental Value less a 10% voids deduction), i.e. ≈1.8% effective on ARV. The Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2024 (Act No. 3 of 2024, Gazette Legal Supplement Part A Vol. 63 No. 53, assented 27 Mar 2024) WAS enacted and cut the residential rate 3% → 2%; IRD declared every Notice of Assessment dated before 6 Mar 2024 null and void and reissued at 2%, payment due 30 Sep 2024. COLLECTION HALTED: the FY2026 Budget Statement (13 Oct 2025, p.22) states "We halted the Property Tax that created anxiety for thousands of people, including the elderly, retired and low-income" (same words in the House Hansard of 13 Oct 2025); the May 2025 announcement of a Property Tax Act repeal is reported in the press only (secondary) and a ttparliament.org search this pass found no Property Tax repeal Bill or Act, so the statute and its 2% rate are read as still on the books while collection is halted — the legal instrument of the halt is not published at a primary source read this pass (data not publicly available — verify at IRD / Ministry of Finance). Budget 2026 (13 Oct 2025) added a separate Landlord Business Surcharge on rental income from 1 Jan 2026 — a levy on landlords, not a property-tax rate. Non-residential statutory rates were not amended by the 2024 Act: commercial 5%, agricultural 1%, industrial 6% where plant and machinery is housed in a building and 3% where it is not (2018 MoF statement to the House — re-verify against the Act before relying on them). Before any carry-cost computation, confirm with IRD whether any property tax is being assessed or collected for the purchase year and whether arrears from 2024 notices remain a charge on the property | FY2026 Budget Statement https://www.finance.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Budget-Statement-FY-2026-1.pdf + House Hansard 13 Oct 2025 https://www.ttparliament.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/hh2025013u.pdf + https://www.ird.gov.tt/news/amended-property-tax-notices-of-assessment + tax.mygovtt.com + Ministry of Finance media release 18 Nov 2023 | 2026-09-24 | 2026-11-15 | 1 | --tax (TT)`

## EU-wide instruments — transposition and application dates

A **directive** binds all 27 member states but reaches a buyer only through national law; the transposition deadline is the date by which that national law must exist. A **regulation** applies directly, so its application date is the date it bites. Every row below was read off the instrument's own EUR-Lex text on the verified date — do not restate a deadline from memory.

| Instrument | Topic | Deadline / application | Status (verified 2026-07-31) | Affected sections |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **(EU) 2024/1275** (EPBD recast, directive) | Energy performance — zero-emission buildings, MEPS, mandatory renovation passports | Transposition **29 May 2026**; art. 17(15) (no financial incentives for stand-alone fossil-fuel boilers) was due earlier, **1 Jan 2025** | **Deadline passed, unmet everywhere** — on 15 Jul 2026 the Commission opened infringement procedures with letters of formal notice to **all 27** member states for failing to fully transpose; two months to respond, then a possible reasoned opinion ([DG ENER, 15 Jul 2026](https://energy.ec.europa.eu/news/commission-calls-eu-countries-transpose-reinforced-rules-energy-performance-buildings-2026-07-15_en)) | `--esg` `--rental` |
| **(EU) 2024/1028** (regulation) | Short-let data collection + registration harmonisation | Directly applicable, **applies 20 May 2026** — a single date, no later tranche; member states had to adopt the penalty provisions by the same date; Commission evaluation due by 20 May 2031 (art. 18) | In application since 20 May 2026 | `--rental` |
| **(EU) 2024/1640** (AMLD6, directive) | Beneficial-ownership registers + access rules; **art. 18 single access point to real-estate information** | Main transposition **10 Jul 2027**. Derogations (art. 78): art. 74 by 10 Jul 2025 · **arts. 11, 12, 13 and 15 — BO-register access, incl. legitimate-interest access — by 10 Jul 2026** · **art. 18 by 10 Jul 2029** | 10 Jul 2026 access tranche now due; main deadline pending | `--tax` (ownership) `--finance` |
| **(EU) 2023/2226** (DAC8, Council directive) | Crypto-asset reporting | Adopt + publish by **31 Dec 2025**, apply from **1 Jan 2026**; two later tranches — 31 Dec 2027 (apply 1 Jan 2028) and 31 Dec 2029 (apply 1 Jan 2030) | First tranche in application since 1 Jan 2026; per-country transposition not individually verified | `--tax` |
| **(EU) 2024/1689** (AI Act, regulation) | High-risk AI — incl. systems that **evaluate creditworthiness or establish a credit score** of natural persons (annex III, 5(b)), i.e. mortgage decisioning | Directly applicable. General application **2 Aug 2026**; chapters I–II from 2 Feb 2025; chapter III section 4, V, VII, XII + art. 78 from 2 Aug 2025; art. 6(1) (the annex I product-safety route) deferred to 2 Aug 2027. Annex III systems are high-risk under **art. 6(2)**, so they follow the general date | Annex III high-risk obligations apply from 2 Aug 2026 | `--finance` |
| **(EU) 2023/1791** (EED recast, directive) | Energy efficiency | Transposition **11 Oct 2025**; applies from 12 Oct 2025 (art. 37 from 30 Jun 2024) | Deadline passed. **Supersedes Directive 2012/27/EU** — which Directive (EU) 2018/2002 amended — repealed with effect from 12 Oct 2025 (art. 39); cite the recast, never 2018/2002 | `--esg` |
| **(EU) 2026/849** (CRR RTS, delegated) | What counts as an **equivalent legal mechanism** — i.e. a completion guarantee — for a residential property **under construction** under CRR art. 124(3)(a)(iii)(2); decides whether a lender may apply the preferential immovable-property risk weight to an off-plan mortgage | n/a — directly applicable, no transposition; **applies 12 Aug 2026** | Adopted 16 Apr 2026 (C/2026/2429), [OJ L, 2026/849, 23.7.2026](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg_del/2026/849/oj/eng); in force 20th day after publication. Built on EBA final draft EBA/RTS/2025/05 (submitted 5 Aug 2025) + EBA opinion EBA/Op/2026/03 (20 Feb 2026). Verified 2026-07-31 | `--finance` `--type=off-plan` |

**Rule**: For any EU country playbook's `--esg` or `--rental` section, EPBD recast 2024/1275 must be referenced if the section claims any energy-rating rental ban schedule. Since 15 Jul 2026 **no member state has fully transposed it** (all 27 hold a letter of formal notice), so never present an EPBD requirement as if it already bound an owner in a given country — cite the *national* law that exists today, and name the directive only as the source of the obligation to legislate. A national rental-ban schedule that predates the recast (e.g. the FR DPE ladder) stands on its own national footing and is unaffected by this.

**Rule**: The AI Act's high-risk obligations for creditworthiness/credit-scoring systems (annex III, 5(b) → art. 6(2)) apply from **2 Aug 2026**. They bind the *provider and deployer* of the scoring system — a bank, not the buyer — so a `--finance` section may note that a mortgage decision made by an automated system now carries transparency and human-oversight duties, but must not imply the buyer gains a direct claim or that a specific lender is non-compliant.

**Rule**: Reg (EU) 2026/849 is a **bank-side prudential rule, not a buyer obligation** — never present it as something the buyer must satisfy. It is relevant only where a `--type=off-plan` or `--finance` brief discusses mortgage availability: art. 1(2) requires the completion guarantee to be one the **member state's own law requires**, so a jurisdiction with no statutory completion guarantee cannot meet the test at all. Whether a given national scheme (FR *garantie financière d'achèvement*, ES *aval bancario* / *seguro de caución*, IT *polizza fideiussoria*, …) satisfies **all** of art. 1(2)–(13) is the lender's assessment — do not assert that it does.

## Per-playbook impact map

When a Tier 1–2 entry is added here, these playbook sections need re-stamping:

| Topic | Sections to re-stamp |
|---|---|
| tax (rate/threshold) | `--tax`, `--rental` if rental-affecting, country playbook benchmark tables |
| visa (open/close) | `--visa`, country playbook `--work` if tied, `shared/visa-programs.md` |
| rental (regulation) | `--rental`, `--tax` if cedolare-style regime |
| esg (energy-rating) | `--esg`, `--rental` (rental-ban schedules) |
| ownership (foreign buyer) | country playbook `--tax` ownership block, `shared/exit.md` foreign-buyer-pool table |
| eurozone | `--currency`, `--tax`, all country benchmarks in local currency |
| cadastre | country playbook `--mains` + `--tax`, `shared/comparable-transactions.md` tier classification |
| pension regime | `shared/retirement.md`, country playbook `--tax` |

## Maintenance cadence

This file gets the same auto-validate treatment as country playbooks:

- **Weekly**: URL liveness on every entry (cron via `auto-validate.md`)
- **Monthly**: scan all entries with `revisit_by` ≤ 30 days from today; create review queue
- **Quarterly**: full re-research of all Tier 1 entries (visa programs especially); confirm ENDED programs haven't been resurrected
- **At-event**: when a regulatory news item lands (EU Official Journal, national gazette, ECJ ruling, central-bank decision), add or update an entry **before** it propagates errors into playbooks

Subscribe these feeds for at-event updates:
- EU Official Journal: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/oj/direct-access.html
- ECJ press releases: https://curia.europa.eu/juris/recherche.jsf?language=en
- Per-country gazettes (LégiFrance, Gazzetta Ufficiale, BOE, etc. — already in country playbooks)
- OECD Tax Policy Reforms annual report
- Tax Foundation Europe weekly digest

---

## Format for new entries

```markdown
- `<effective_date> | <topic> | <one-line summary> | <source name> | <verified_date> | <revisit_by> | <tier> | <sections>`
```

Multi-line is fine if the entry is complex; the seven-pipe format is the canonical machine-readable form.

- `shared/visa-programs.md` — universal `--visa` section; ENDED registry above is the source of truth
